=== [commands/REPORT-CP1252] confusing code-defect :: `report > pr5-report.md` crashes and leaves a 0-byte file if any blocker contains a character outside cp1252
    VOTES: reproduce:confirmed(repro) refute:confirmed(repro)
    section: 5 (and any non-JSON redirect)
    CLAIM: cli._print uses print(). When stdout is redirected in cmd, Python 3.13 writes it as cp1252 with errors=strict. Blockers carry raw CLI and model text: Cursor result and stderr lines, Codex and Claude error text, gh stderr (gh's own output uses U+2713). One non-cp1252 character makes `%C% report > pr5-report.md` exit 1 with a traceback and an EMPTY report, which is the file the owner is asked to send. The --json outputs are safe because json.dumps escapes non-ASCII. The current ledger is ASCII, so this does not happen today.
    EVIDENCE: cli.py:30-37. In cmd, python stdout redirected reports encoding cp1252 (cp1252 locale). On a copy with a pr5-cursor blocker 'tool call failed → retry; ✓ checked': `%C% report > pr5-report.md` exited 1 with UnicodeEncodeError: 'charmap' codec can't encode character '→' in position 648, and pr5-report.md was 0 bytes. `%C% status > file` exited 1 the same way. `%C% --json status > file` exited 0 (4036 bytes). With `set PYTHONIOENCODING=utf-8`, report exited 0 and the file ended with 'Journal rows 5'. Sources of such text: cursor.py:493 and 505, codex.py:573, claude.py:488-497, lifecycle.py:276.
    SHEET EDIT: Section 1: add `set PYTHONIOENCODING=utf-8` after the other set lines.
    CODE CHANGE: In cli.main, before dispatch: `for s in (sys.stdout, sys.stderr): getattr(s, 'reconfigure', None) and s.reconfigure(errors='backslashreplace')`. Or use encoding='utf-8' when not s.isatty().
    [reproduce confirmed] I reproduced the finding independently on my own copy of the pr5 ledger. I ran the commands through `cmd.exe /d /s /c` with stdout redirected, the way the sheet's section 5 does. PYTHONIOENCODING, PYTHONUTF8 and PYTHONLEGACYWINDOWSSTDIO were removed from the child's environment.

Why it matters for the owner's window:
- On this host none of those three variables is set at user or machine level. PYTHONIOENCODING is set only inside this Claude process (to utf-8:surrogateescape), so a fresh Command Prompt does not have it.
- The system ANSI code page is 1252.
- In that environment a redirected Python stdout reports encoding cp1252.

What the code does:
- `cli._print` (cli.py:30-37) prints the whole report with one `print()`. `report` builds it at cli.py:349-353 through `metrics.render_report`. `_render_task` (metrics.py:162) writes `- Blocker: {t['blocker']}` verbatim, and `_format_status` 
      corrected_claim: The claim holds, with two corrections.
- The redirected stdout's errors handler on this Python 3.13 is 'surrogateescape', not 'strict'. It still raises UnicodeEncodeError for any character cp1252 cannot encode that is not a surrogate.
- I did not show that gh's U+2713 reaches a blocker. The likelier sources are model and CLI text (Cursor and Codex stderr, Claude's result_text), which goes verbatim into blockers at cursor.py:493 and 505, codex.py:573 and claude.py:488-497.

What triggers it: arrows, check marks, box-drawing characters, emoji, CJK and the like. Characters cp1252 can encode (em dash, curly quotes, ellipsis, bullet) do not.

What fails and what does not:
- `report > file` and `status > file` exit 1 and leave a 0-byte file.
- The `--json` forms of status, metrics and report are safe.
- Console output with no redirect is safe, so sections 1 and 2 are unaffected.
      corrected_fix: Sheet edit, section 1: add `set PYTHONIOENCODING=utf-8` after the `set ASHLAR_CONTROLLER_POLICY=...` line. I verified it: `report > file` exits 0 with the non-cp1252 blocker present.

As a belt-and-braces step, section 5 could also keep `%C% --json report > pr5-report.json`, which survives without the env var.

Code change, in cli.main before `parser = build_parser()`:
```python
for s in (sys.stdout, sys.stderr):
    if hasattr(s, 'reconfigure') and not s.isatty():
        s.reconfigure(encoding='utf-8')
```
I verified this on a scratch copy of the package: `report > file` and `status > file` exit 0, and the emoji is written as UTF-8.

The finding's alternative, `reconfigure(errors='backslashreplace')`, also prevents the crash, but it writes the escape text (`→`) instead of the character.

Code (cli.main, before parse/dispatch). The utf-8 variant is preferred because it keeps the text faithful in a Markdown file; backslashreplace is the fallback for stray lone surrogates:

```python
for s in (sys.stdout,):
    if s is not None and hasattr(s, "reconfigure") and not s.isatty():
        s.reconfigure(encoding="utf-8", errors="backslashreplace")
```

Leave stderr alone; it is already backslashreplace. io.StringIO has no reconfigure method, so the hasattr guard keeps any in-process capture safe.

      corrected_fix: CODE (the proposed flags are right; refinements):

(a) Put the spawn options in one helper in adapters/base.py and call it from all three adapters:
```
_CREATE_NEW_PROCESS_GROUP = 0x00000200
_CREATE_NO_WINDOW = 0x08000000

def detached_spawn_kwargs(windows):
    return ({"creationflags": _CREATE_NEW_PROCESS_GROUP | _CREATE_NO_WINDOW} if windows
            else {"start_new_session": True})
```
Use literal values, not subprocess.CREATE_*: those attributes do not exist on Linux, so a test that forces windows=True would fail there.

(b) CREATE_NO_WINDOW is the flag that matters. It gives the worker its own hidden console, so neither Ctrl+C nor closing the window reaches it, and the worker's own child processes (PowerShell, node, bash) share that hidden console instead of opening new windows. CREATE_NEW_PROCESS_GROUP alone would block Ctrl+C but not window close. Do not use DETACHED_PROCESS:

=== [sequencing/SEQ-1] wrong-outcome code-defect :: Every CLI-driven Cursor run ends without an exit code, so a successful run settles as no-final-result (or rate-limited) and never opens a PR
    VOTES: reproduce:confirmed(repro) refute:confirmed(repro)
    section: 3
    CLAIM: Each `tick` is its own Python process. The Cursor adapter records a run's exit code only through the Popen object held by the process that launched it (cursor.py:421-435). Every later tick polls from a fresh adapter, sees only the pid, and gets rc None. In _classify (cursor.py:495-509), a clean finish therefore never reaches the `rc == 0 -> completed` rule. The quota, rate-limit and transient patterns are matched against stderr plus the whole stdout stream, and otherwise the result is `no-final-result`. So the first logged-in Cursor run will settle `no-final-result`: the task goes to needs-reconcile, the full 12 requests are charged, and no commit or PR happens. If the model's own output mentions 'rate limit', 'timed out' or 'usage limit', it settles rate-limited, transient or quota-exhausted instead, and the task is re-queued after 60 s or 1 h. That re-queue feeds SEQ-2, or a second Cursor run if section-3 ticks continue. Codex is not affected, because codex.py:530-595 classifies from `turn.completed` in the stream.

      corrected_claim: Every Cursor run launched by a CLI `tick` is polled by a later `tick` process through a new CursorAdapter that holds no Popen, so its exit code is never recorded (cursor.py:421-435). - `no-final-result` by default: the task goes to needs-reconcile.

=== [evidence/E1] wrong-outcome code-defect :: Cursor exit codes are never recorded, so a successful Cursor run settles as no-final-result (or is misread as transient/quota and launched again)
    VOTES: refute:confirmed(repro) reproduce:confirmed(repro)
    section: 3
    CLAIM: Every `tick` in the sheet is its own process. The tick that launches Cursor returns straight after Popen (cursor.py:417), and the tick that settles the run builds a fresh CursorAdapter whose `_procs` is empty. In that case `_alive` only checks whether the pid exists (cursor.py:431-435), so `manifest.exit` is never filled. `_classify` treats a run as completed only when `rc == 0` (cursor.py:495). With rc None, a clean run falls through to the failure texts (cursor.py:500-505), then to `no-final-result` with the blocker `the exit code of the run was not recorded` (cursor.py:507-509). The task goes to needs-reconcile and no PR opens. If the stream contains any _QUOTA/_RATE_LIMIT/_TRANSIENT word (the controller README alone has 5 quota, 7 rate-limit and 1 network hits), the clean run is instead classified quota-exhausted, rate-limited or transient. For transient that means waiting-rate-limit for 60 s, and the next `--adapter cursor` tick launches a SECOND Cursor run in a fresh clone. This undoes the module's own integrator decision that 'exit 0 with non-empty stdout is completed BEFORE the quota, rate-limit and transient texts'. Every unit test polls through the adapter that launched the run (test_cursor_adapter.py:118-123, 494-500), so none covers this. Codex has the same evidence loss: result_json.exit is always null in this flow, though its classification reads the stream and is unaffected. Evidence consequence: the first logged-in Cursor run cannot yield 'a successful run's exit code', which the module doc lists as unseen. result_text, event_types and session_id_candidates are still kept (see E11).

      corrected_claim: Confirmed as stated. Three further consequences:

      corrected_fix: Code (cursor.py):
1. Spawn through an ISOLATED waiter: `[sys.executable, '-I', '-c', _WAITER, os.path.join(run_dir, 'exit.json'), '--'] + argv`.
   - `-I` matters because the waiter's cwd is the workspace the model edits, and it also ignores PYTHON* variables.
   - The waiter sets `signal.signal(signal.SIGINT, signal.SIG_IGN)`, so a Ctrl+C in the owner's console does not kill it before it records node's exit.
   - It runs `p = subprocess.Popen(sys.argv[3:], stdin=DEVNULL); rc = p.wait()`, writes {'exit': rc} to exit.json.tmp, then os.replace onto exit.json, then exits with `rc if 0 <= rc < 256 else 1`. Windows NTSTATUS codes do not fit a process exit status; the JSON keeps the true value.
   - stdout and stderr are inherited, so they still land in stdout.json and stderr.log. kill_tree's `/T` (and killpg on POSIX) still covers the whole tree.
2. In `_alive`: when no Popen is held, the pid
    [reproduce confirmed] I reproduced the defect in two processes, as the sheet's CLI ticks run it. Everything ran against fresh copies of the pr5 ledger in my scratch dir, with the test suite's fake_cursor_cli.py. I never called Controller.tick(), ran no real CLI and used no gh.

    [refute confirmed] I read the code end to end and found nothing that makes the claim false. There is no other guard and no other way the transfer gets built, and the docs do not describe this behaviour as intended.

1. `run list` only reads the ledger (cli.py:432-434). `status` and `report` only read it too (controller.py:715-758). A run's state moves only when something polls it:
   - a tick, through settle_and_reconcile (controller.py:279-314, called at :479);
   - the transfer itself (controller.py:393-417);
   - the manual `run settle` / `run cancel`, which the sheet never uses.
   So a Codex process that has already exited still shows `running`.

2. The transfer runs in a new process, so `CodexAdapter._procs` is empty. Polling there falls back to `pid_alive(manifest pid)` (codex.py:486-499, store.py:217-242):

The code produces four outcomes, not the sheet's two:
- **(a)** process alive: stopped [{how: cancelled, stop_reason: cancelled, charged: 12}], state queued;
- **(b)** process exited: stopped [{how: settled, stop_reason: whatever the stream says}], state queued. With `completed` this is D1: a task-state `done` just before the move, and no PR;

      corrected_claim: The facts are confirmed as stated. Two corrections:
1. Severity is "confusing", not "blocks-run". The literal gate `codex login status` at live-run-pr5.md:16 fails in a fresh cmd.exe because nothing named codex is on the registry PATH (the handoff itself says so at line 325). But the controller does not depend on that gate: it resolves the app binary and runs its own `login status` route check before any spawn (codex.py:364-387, 413-415). A failed or skipped gate therefore spends nothing. Code paths:
- `_eligible_task` (controller.py:447-463) walks the queued and waiting-* tasks in `ORDER BY priority, created_at, task_id`. It skips a task only when its next_eligible_at is in the future, when its policy `enabled` flag is false, or when its provider is in the `disabled_providers` control. It never checks `self.adapters`.
- `tick` (controller.py:522-532) takes that first task. If no adapter is registered for its provider, it returns `blocked provider-disabled` with "no adapter registered for provider X". It writes no state and does not fall through to the next task, so every later tick repeats the same block.
- `lifecycle.phase()` (lifecycle.py:413-431) only observes open PRs. With `--gh cli` there is no other dispatch path.
- README.md:68-7
      corrected_claim: After section 3, pr5-cursor can be left dispatchable with cursor operator-enabled. === [transfer-path/T5] wrong-outcome risk :: A reused PID makes a finished run poll as running, and the transfer's cancel then runs taskkill /T /F on an unrelated process tree
    VOTES: reproduce:confirmed(repro) refute:confirmed(repro)
    section: 4. One transfer
    CLAIM: Across processes, 'is the run alive' is pid_alive(manifest pid) alone (codex.py:496-499, cursor.py:431-435, claude.py:357-361; store.py:217-251 only checks STILL_ACTIVE). Under this condition: the Codex process has exited and Windows has given its PID to another process before `task transfer` runs. Then poll returns RUNNING for a completed run, and transfer_task calls cancel_run (controller.py:416). The adapter writes cancelled_at, so the completed work is recorded as cancelled, and it runs `taskkill /T /F /PID <pid>` (codex.py:600-611), which kills the unrelated process and everything it started. claude.py:358-359 acknowledges the reused-pid limit for polling, but the PR 5 transfer turns that poll into a kill. The window is the time between the run's exit and the transfer, which is short in the sheet but not zero. A related race, derived from the code: a run that exits between the poll and the cancel is also recorded as cancelled.
    EVIDENCE: xproc.out C5: a manifest pointing at a live unrelated python process (my own victim, with a child) and a stream that already has turn.completed -> poll from a fresh CodexAdapter 'running'; cancel -> 'unrelated process and its child killed by cancel': true.
    SHEET EDIT: The tick-first procedure in T2 never polls a finished run from the transfer process, so it removes the exposure in the finished branch. In the running branch, add `tasklist /FI "PID eq <pid from manifest.json>"` and transfer only if it lists codex.exe.
    CODE CHANGE: At launch, record the child's creation time in the manifest: on Windows, GetProcessTimes through OpenProcess(PROCESS_QUERY_LIMITED_INFORMATION); on POSIX, /proc/<pid>/stat field 22.       corrected_fix: **Code** (all three adapters, plus controller.cancel_run):
1. **Record the process identity at launch.** Right after Popen, read the creation time from the Popen's own handle: on Windows, `GetProcessTimes(proc._handle)`, which is race-free because the handle pins the process.
